# Frontend overview

What the Angular app contains today and the details a change must keep. The rules are in `.claude/rules/frontend.md` and `testing.md`; the HTTP shapes in `docs/api-contract.md`.

## Shell, routing, auth

- `src/index.html`: title, meta description, `<link rel="canonical">` and Open Graph tags (`og:url`, `og:image` `https://nfnn.cz/images/og-image.jpg` 1200×630 JPEG, a placeholder cut from the hero until real content arrives, `twitter:card`); `core/seo/canonical-link.ts` (`CanonicalLink.start()` from an app initializer, `SITE_ORIGIN` `https://nfnn.cz`) rewrites the canonical link and `og:url` to the path of every navigation without query or fragment; `public/robots.txt` keeps crawlers out of `/api/`, `/mcp`, `/darce/`, `/sprava/` and the link pages and names `public/sitemap.xml` (home and the privacy policy; news and project pages wait for a generated sitemap); icons (`favicon.svg` is the only linked tab icon, everything that can be SVG on the site is SVG, Jakub 2026-10-30; `favicon.ico`, linked from nowhere, answers services that ask only the default address `/favicon.ico`: without it claude.ai showed the DuckDNS duck for the connector, the icon of the parent domain; `apple-touch-icon.png`, which iOS takes only as PNG; `app-icon.svg`, linked from nowhere, is the icon for the ZimaOS app list, set there as its icon URL since 2026-10-30); loads nothing from other sites. Launch (#348): `<app-root>` holds a static launch screen (`.launch-screen`, `src/styles/_launch-screen.scss`: the theme's `icon-512.png` at `--launch-icon-size` in the middle of `--color-surface`), which the page paints before any script runs and Angular replaces with the first page; `apple-mobile-web-app-capable` (with `mobile-web-app-capable`, which keeps Chrome from warning) makes iOS fetch the `apple-touch-startup-image` links when the site is added to the Home Screen (iOS 27 fetched none without it), one per iPhone and iPad screen and orientation of `STARTUP_SCREENS` in `scripts/make-startup-images.mjs`, which the `postbuild` of `npm run build` draws into `startup/<width>x<height>.png` of the build from the built manifest's `background_color`, `icon-512.png` and the built stylesheet's `--launch-icon-size` (no library: it reads and writes the PNGs itself), so the launch image iOS shows at once equals the page's first paint and a theme brings its own; `scripts/make-startup-images.test.mjs` keeps the links of `index.html` equal to that list. The images are not in `ngsw-config.json`: iOS keeps its own copy from the moment of adding, so an installed app shows a changed launch image only after it is added again. `main.ts` bootstraps and references `@angular/localize`. `styles.scss` loads the theme, reset, keyframes; the scrollbars are the browser's own (Jakub 2026-11-05: no custom scrollbar styles).
- Zoom (Jakub 2026-10-31, plan v3 2.23d): the viewport meta in `src/index.html` is `width=device-width, initial-scale=1, maximum-scale=1, viewport-fit=cover` (Android Chrome and the desktop emulators then never pinch-zoom the page), `html { touch-action: manipulation }` in `styles.scss` stops double-tap zoom, and `core/zoom/page-pinch-lock.ts` (`PagePinchLock.start()` from the app initializer `pagePinchLockAtStart`) cancels Safari's `gesturestart`, `gesturechange` and `gestureend` on the document (non-passive), because Safari on the iPhone and iPad ignores the viewport limit; no `touchmove` listener, so scrolling never waits on script. The text size still follows the browser and the system (rem tokens); in the app opened from the home screen of an iPhone or iPad, which has no aA menu, `core/zoom/system-text-size.ts` (`SystemTextSize.start()` from the app initializer `systemTextSizeAtStart`) puts `follows-system-text-size` on `html` when `navigator.standalone` is `true` on a touch screen (a Mac's Dock app has none), and `styles.scss` gives that root `font: -apple-system-body` with `font-family: var(--font-sans)`, so the root size is the iPhone's text size setting (Dynamic Type, 17 px at the default; Jakub 2026-11-05, Q13); Safari in a tab and every other browser keep 16 px and their own text size. Playwright's WebKit has no Dynamic Type, so the size itself is a device check. Photos and the Přispět QR open in `shared/ui/zoom-viewer`. At the largest system text (53 px, AX5) the fluid heading tokens shrink to what the phone's width allows (`max(1rem, min(<rem>, 16vw))` as their minimum, so a long Czech word still fits a phone's line; unchanged at the default size from 320 px), `h1`–`h3` break a word only when it cannot fit (`overflow-wrap: break-word`, in `styles.scss`), the sign-in dialog's Přihlášení / Registrace switch stacks its buttons (`flex-wrap`), and a dialog's × keeps the size of its fixed button (`--text-dialog-close`); `e2e/largest-text.spec.ts` checks the header, the headings and the switch at 53 px on 320 and 375 px.
- Touch (#380, #385, 2026-11-06): a touch answers as in a native iOS app. `src/styles/_controls.scss` (loaded by `styles.scss` before the `ui-` blocks) removes Safari's grey tap flash (`-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent` on `html`) and makes every control (`a[href]`, `button`, `summary`, `[role="button"]`, `[role="tab"]`) unselectable and undraggable, without the long-press callout (no link preview with "Otevřít / Přidat do seznamu četby", no "Zkopírovat / Vyhledat" over a label), and decorative images (`img[alt=""]`) and images inside controls without callout and drag; links to another site, `mailto:` and `tel:` keep the callout, so the address can still be copied or shared, and links without a class inside running text (`p` other than the crumbs, `dd`, `address`, `app-markdown`) stay selectable with the system menu, as all content text, the bank details and every field do. A pressed state on `:active` (`src/styles/_pressed.scss`: `when-pressed` for an enabled control, `look`, which under `forced-colors` becomes a 2 px `Highlight` outline inside the control): by default a tint of the control's own text colour (`--color-pressed`, 8 % in `nova-nadeje`) laid as a background image over its background, with a weight (`:active:active` in `_controls.scss`) that wins over a component's base rule but loses to its own `:active` rule and to the `ui-` blocks; the solid `.ui-button` and `.ui-cta` darken (`--pressed-brightness`), `.ui-cta--ghost` and the rows of `.ui-list` tint, a card that its link covers (news, featured news, project) shrinks (`--pressed-scale` in `--duration-fast`, back in its `--duration-base`; under `prefers-reduced-motion` it darkens instead) and a tab of the tab bar shows the pill (`--color-tab-pill`, whose label contrast `check-themes.mjs` already proves). Safari on the iPhone applies `:active` only while the document listens to touches, so `core/touch/pressed-on-touch.ts` (`PressedOnTouch.start()` from the app initializer `pressedOnTouchAtStart`) keeps a passive no-op `touchstart` listener on the document. Hover looks stand only inside `@media (hover: hover)`, so a tap leaves none behind (a card no longer stays lifted); the stylelint rule `nfnn/hover-only-with-hover` (`scripts/stylelint-hover-media.mjs`, run by `npm run lint:styles`, tested by `stylelint-hover-media.test.mjs`) refuses a `:hover` outside it. `e2e/touch-controls.spec.ts` checks what selecting a page takes and leaves; the callout and the pressed look are device checks, since Playwright's browsers know no `-webkit-touch-callout`.
- On an iPhone Safari 26 lets the page show under its status bar (Jakub 2026-11-05, replacing the blue strip of 2026-10-30). Safari 26 ignores `<meta name="theme-color">`, and the site sets none: WebKit (`LocalFrameView::fixedContainerEdges`) hit-tests the middle of the top edge 4 px inside the viewport, ignoring `pointer-events`, and walks up to the first fixed or sticky box at least 90 % as wide as the viewport (it skips one with `visibility: hidden`, opacity under 0.1, or 10 px or thinner). Without such a box, or when that box or anything on the way to it has a `backdrop-filter` (`PredominantColorType::Multiple`), it leaves the bar untinted with the page under it; otherwise it tints the bar with the first plain `background-color` on the way, and a fixed box without one is sampled once from the pixels under it and kept. The app header is `position: absolute` and the tab bar sits at the bottom, so no page has such a box at the top edge; a new fixed or sticky bar at the top keeps it that way with a `backdrop-filter` or changes the status bar with it. Chrome on Android shows its default toolbar. `e2e/status-bar.spec.ts` checks the top edge at 390 px on `/`, `/projekty` and the privacy policy (opened and scrolled) and on the donor overview, and that the page paints no tint and names no theme colour. `app.ts` renders `<router-outlet />` and the sign-in dialog while `LoginDialogService.isOpen()` (Back closes it, and goes back a step first from the code from the app and from the recovery by payment, through `DialogHistory`; a sign-in with `returnTo` hands its entry over to that page, the privacy policy link in the registration replaces it) (re-created on every open so panels start clean, except a half-filled registration that `LoginDialogService` keeps without its password until a sign-in or a sent registration; its "Přihlášení / Registrace" switch carries `aria-pressed` and shows only on those two starting steps). `app.config.ts`: `provideRouter` with `chunkLoadRecoveryFeature` (`withNavigationErrorHandler` handing every navigation error to `ChunkLoadRecovery`, below) and `withInMemoryScrolling` (anchor scrolling for `routerLink="/o-nas" fragment="o-nas"` links; the sections carry Czech ids without diacritics: `o-nas` `o-nas`, `kontakt` `aktuality`, `/o-nas`, `kontakt`), `provideHttpClient(withXhr(), withInterceptors([authInterceptor]))` (`appHttpClient`; XMLHttpRequest because the fetch backend reports no upload progress, which the backup upload of the first setup shows), session restore on start.
- `core/reload/` and `core/navigation/`: `reload-guard.ts` (`ReloadGuard.claim(reason)`, `ReloadReason` `'chunk-load'`, `'update'`, `'unrecoverable'`): one automatic reload per reason and 60 s (`RELOAD_WINDOW_MS`), the time kept in `sessionStorage` under `nfnn.reload.<reason>` (reached through the `SESSION_STORAGE` token); when the storage cannot be read, written or read back it allows none, so a tab can never reload in a loop. `chunk-load-recovery.ts`: `isChunkLoadError(error)` recognises a failed dynamic import by the browsers' messages (Chromium and Firefox "dynamically imported module", Safari "Importing a module script failed", the MIME-type refusals); `ChunkLoadRecovery.handleNavigationError` sets `failure` (`{ url, initial }`, `initial` while the router has shown no page yet, `Router.navigated`), kept until the next successful navigation, and loads the page that was asked for in the newest build (`AppUpdates.loadNewVersion` of the navigation's URL, which asks the service worker for the new build first) only when the browser is online (`Connectivity.browserSaysOffline()`, `navigator.onLine`), `ChunkProbe` says the reload would land and the guard allows it; `failure` is set before that load, so a browser that stays on the page (a cancelled unload) still shows the offer; `reload()` is the visitor's own reload of it. `chunk-probe.ts`: `ChunkProbe.reloadWouldLand(error)` asks the server with `fetch(…, { method: 'HEAD', cache: 'no-store', signal })` for the chunk the error names (a same-origin `.js`/`.mjs` address in the message), aborted after 4 s (`PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS`), and answers yes, when nothing redirected the request, for a 404 or an HTML page at the chunk's own address (a deploy), or, when the message names none (Safari), for an HTML answer of `/`; another answer, a redirect, a rejected or aborted request (the network is gone or hangs while `navigator.onLine` still says online) is no, so the recovery only offers the reload. `handleNavigationError` sets `failure` before it asks, counts the router's `NavigationStart` events and ignores an answer that comes after another navigation started. It sees only router navigations: a failed `import()` outside the router, a `@defer` block or a worker gets `ChunkLoadRecovery.handleImportFailure` in 1.9, which offers a reload and never reloads by itself (Phase 0 gate, J-m1). `ExternalNavigation.loadFromServer(url)` goes there with `location.assign`, or, when only the fragment differs from the current document (`/#prispet` on `/`), which `assign` would treat as a jump within the page, replaces the address with `history.replaceState` and reloads. Why: `docs/system-overview.md`, Frontend.
- `core/connectivity/` (plan v3 2.7): `Connectivity` (root, started by `connectivityAtStart` in `app.config.ts`) decides from what the API answers whether the app is offline, never from `navigator.onLine` alone. `connectivityInterceptor` (the last interceptor) reports every `/api` outcome: an answer of the server (any status, or a gateway status with an `application/problem+json` body) counts as reached, while status 0 and 502, 503, 504 or 520–530 without a problem body (`isUnreachable`) count as failed; after a failure it probes at once and goes offline after 3 s (`OFFLINE_AFTER_MS`) without a success, then probes every 30 s (`PROBE_EVERY_MS`) while the tab is visible, at the window's `online` event and when the tab becomes visible again; the window's `offline` event and an answer the service worker served from its cache (`mayComeFromServiceWorker`, with a `Date` more than 15 s older than the server's clock, which it learns from network answers) only start a probe; a fresher one counts as contact while the app is online, but once it is offline it too only starts a probe, since a copy kept seconds ago reached no server. The probe (`connectivity-probe-api.ts`) is `GET /api/operations/setup` without the token, so it bypasses the service worker, with a 10 s timeout. `cause` is `network` for status 0, a 504 of the worker or a browser that says offline, else `server`; `tryAgain()` probes on request. `shown-answers.ts` (`ShownAnswers`) keeps the `Date` of each public answer the current page shows (reset at a navigation to another path), whose oldest is the time in the label; `data-time.ts` writes it as `9:30` today, `4. 11. 9:30` yesterday, else a day (`15. 3. 2026`), without leading zeros except the minutes (Jakub 2026-11-06). `unseen-photos.ts` (`UnseenPhotos`, started with it) marks an `<img>` that fails to load `is-unavailable` (the global style makes it transparent, so its box and its text stay and no broken-image sign shows) and takes the mark off when it loads; offline that is a photo the service worker never kept, since it keeps only photos already seen, in the size the screen asked for, up to 300 (Jakub 2026-11-05, Q8). `browserSaysOffline()` replaces the former `core/navigation/connectivity.ts` for the error reporter and the chunk-load recovery.
- `core/device/crypto/` (plan v3, 2.13; called by the store of 2.14, and by the administration copy from 2.19): `copy-keys.ts` (`OpenCopy.create()`, `sealRecord`/`openRecord` at a `RecordAddress`, `wrapWithPassword`/`wrapWithPrf`/`wrapWithSessionKey`, `rekeyed()`, which closes the old copy at once, so a caller reads every record first, re-keys, then seals and writes them all again, outside one IndexedDB transaction, which would commit across the WebCrypto awaits; `close()`; `openWithPassword`, `openWithPrf`, `openWithSessionKey`, `generateSessionKey`; refusals as `CopyRefused` `wrong-secret`, `wrong-wrap`, `damaged`, `closed`), `password-key.ts` (`deriveWithMeasuredParameters`: Argon2id 128 MiB when the worker timed it within 1 s, else 64 MiB; `WorkerPasswordKeyDerivation` starts `argon2.worker.ts` per derivation, 30 s timeout; the messages in `password-key-protocol.ts`, which the worker imports), `argon2.ts` (hash-wasm, only for the worker and tests), `prf.ts` (`prfInput` from the device salt; `readPrf` over the device's passkeys or any of the site, giving the passkey, its user handle and the output, which the caller zeroes), `device-registry.ts` (`lookupTag`, `userHandleTag`, `registryEntry`, no e-mail in the clear), `hkdf.ts`, `device-bytes.ts` (`framed` length-prefixed fields for AAD and HKDF info). How it is secured: `docs/security-overview.md`, "Device copies".
- `core/version/` (plan v3 2.3): `api-generation.ts` (`API_GENERATION`, the server API generation the build speaks, 1), kept equal to `api-generation.json` by its spec, which the backend's `ApiGenerationTest` keeps equal to `VersionHeadersFilter.API_GENERATION`; the same files hold `POSTS_ACROSS_GENERATIONS` (the session refresh, the sign-out and the receipt status question), which both the interceptor and the filter let through, kept equal the same way; `server-version.ts` (`ServerVersion.observe(headers)` keeps the latest `Nfnn-Api` that is a whole number from 1 to 999 999 999 and the latest `Nfnn-Revision` the server would accept from a report (`revisionOf`, or `dev`), ignores anything else; signals `revision`, `apiGeneration`, `mustUpdate`, true while the latest generation differs from `APP_API_GENERATION`, a token that defaults to `API_GENERATION`); `version-interceptor.ts` (`versionInterceptor`, after `authInterceptor` in `app.config.ts`, so it also sees the refresh and the retried call): on every `/api/` call it sets `Nfnn-App: <APP_REVISION>` and `Nfnn-App-Api: <APP_API_GENERATION>` and reads the answer's headers, a refusal's too (not a status 0), which `ServerVersion` takes only from an answer whose `Cache-Control` says `no-store` (a cached answer may carry an old server's headers); while `mustUpdate`, a `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH` or `DELETE` other than `/api/donors/auth/refresh`, `/api/donors/auth/logout` and `/api/donations/receipts/status` is not sent and fails with an `HttpErrorResponse` 426 whose body is the problem `https://nfnn.cz/problems/app/update-required` (`UPDATE_REQUIRED`), title "Web mezitím dostal novou verzi", detail "Nic jsme neodeslali. Načtěte stránku znovu a zkuste to ještě jednou." (`layout.update.problem.*`). Why: `docs/system-overview.md`, Frontend.
- `core/security/trusted-types.ts` (plan v3, 2.5): `installScriptUrlPolicy(trustedTypes, document.baseURI)`, run by `install-trusted-types.ts`, the first import of `main.ts` (so before any other module of the app is evaluated), creates the Trusted Types policy `default` with only `createScriptURL`: `admittedScriptUrl(input, sink, base)` gives the worker constructor (`WORKER_SINK`) an address of the site's own origin, without user name, query or fragment, whose path is `/worker-<8 characters>.js` (the receipt PDF and QR reader workers as the build names them), the service worker registration (`SERVICE_WORKER_SINK`) only `/ngsw-worker.js`, each resolved, and `null` (refused) for any other address or sink; without `trustedTypes`, with a default policy already there or when the browser refuses the policy, nothing is installed and the app starts anyway. The Content-Security-Policy that requires it is in `nginx-security-headers.conf` (`docs/security-overview.md`, Browser and transport).
- `core/live/` (plan v3 2.16): `live-api.ts` asks `GET /api/live` as a text stream (`observe: 'events'`, `reportProgress`), `event-stream.ts` (`EventStreamReader`) cuts the growing text into events (`event:` and `data:` lines, comments skipped), and `live-updates.ts` (`LiveUpdates`, root) keeps one stream while a donor is signed in, a page listens (`changes(feeds)`, which emits on a hint of one of those feeds and once after a reopening that follows a stream lost while it listened, since hints in between are lost; a page that started listening while the stream waited for a retry is told too, since hints of the wait are lost to it, while a page whose arrival opened the stream again has just read everything and is not told) and the tab was not hidden for 30 s; `connected` says whether the revision event of the open stream has arrived. A stream that streamed for 10 s and ended opens again within a second, a failure after 1 s doubling up to 60 s with jitter, a 401 or 403 (after the interceptor's one refresh) not before the session is renewed (a new sign-in or the next token refresh). A stream that streamed for 10 s and then failed starts its retries again from 1 s; each attempt of the request (`Sent`, such as the interceptor's retry after a refresh) is read from its own start. `authInterceptor` reads a problem from a text body too (`application/problem+json` as text), so the stream's 401 `auth/expired-token` refreshes like any call. Specs get `testing/live-fixtures.ts` (`FakeLiveUpdates`: `hint(feed)`, `resync()`, `connected`, `listeners()`) from `src/test-providers.ts`, so no spec opens a stream unless it asks for the real service.
- `core/updates/` and the service worker (plan v3 2.4): `angular-service-worker.ts` (`provideAppServiceWorker()`, in `app.config.ts`) registers Angular's `ngsw-worker.js` (`SERVICE_WORKER_SCRIPT`, a relative name, which the Trusted Types policy resolves against the base URL) with `registerWhenStable:30000`, off in development, and hands `SwUpdate` to the rest of the app as `SERVICE_WORKER_UPDATES` (`service-worker-updates.ts`, `NO_SERVICE_WORKER` without one, so specs and a browser without service workers take the same path). `app-updates.ts` (`AppUpdates.start()` from an app initializer) decides when a ready version (`VERSION_READY`) takes over: right away only within `START_UP_WINDOW_MS` (10 s) of the start, before the first `pointerdown`, `keydown`, `scroll`, `wheel` or `touchstart`, while the page is still at the address it started at (a page that changed its own address, as one that took a link code off it, counts as touched) and with no half-typed form; otherwise at the next router navigation to another path (a change of only the query or the section, `?strana=2` or `#o-nas`, stays in the app), as a full load of that address (`ExternalNavigation.loadFromServer`), and when a half-typed form is on the page the navigation runs in the app and the load follows only once the form is gone; every automatic load is one `ReloadGuard.claim('update')`. An answer naming another build than the app's (`ServerVersion.revision` against `APP_REVISION`) asks the worker to look for an update once per build. `VERSION_INSTALLATION_FAILED` reports `sw-install`/`service-worker`; `SwUpdate.unrecoverable` (a file of the tab's version gone from the server and from the cache) reports `sw-unrecoverable`/`service-worker` and reloads once (`'unrecoverable'`), or sets `mustReload`, which shows the update notice, when a form is half-typed or the guard refuses. `loadNewVersion(url)` asks the worker for the new build (at most `UPDATE_CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`, 10 s, a failure ignored) and then loads `url` from the server; the update notice and `ChunkLoadRecovery` load through it. `dirty-forms.ts` (`DirtyForms.register(check)`, `any()`, a throwing check counts as dirty) and `tracked-form.ts` (`form[appTrackedForm]`, a host directive of `appFocusFirstInvalid`, so every form of the site registers `NgForm.dirty`; the administration's settings form, which has no `appFocusFirstInvalid`, carries it itself). `boot-report.ts` (`markCodeArrived(document)`, the first statement of `main.ts`, sets `data-nfnn-loading` on `<html>`, so the watchdog knows the code arrived and the start only waits; `BootReport.started()`, called by `main.ts` once the app is bootstrapped) sets `data-nfnn-started` on `<html>` and, when `public/boot-guard.js` had to step in during this tab's start (`sessionStorage` `nfnn.boot.recovered`), reports `boot`/`start-up` once. `public/boot-guard.js` (a plain script of the build, loaded by `index.html` before the app, allowed by the policy as `'self'`): when the app has not set `data-nfnn-started` 15 s after the page began, it waits in 15 s steps while `data-nfnn-loading` is set (the code arrived and the start waits on the network, such as a session refresh during an outage) up to 60 s and then shows the panel without a reload; when the code never arrived, it reloads once per 60 s (`nfnn.reload.boot`, never while `navigator.onLine` is false) and otherwise shows the full-screen panel "Web se nenačetl" (`role="alert"`, the button "Vyčistit a načíst znovu" unregisters every service worker and deletes every cache, never `localStorage`, IndexedDB or the cookies, so the sign-in and the data kept on the device stay), which goes away by itself if the app starts after all; its Czech text has no i18n ids, since it runs before Angular; `scripts/boot-guard.test.mjs` tests it in a jsdom page. `ngsw-config.json`: the shell (`index.html`, `boot-guard.js`, the manifest, the tab icon, `icon-512.png` of the launch screen, the logos, every `main-`, `polyfills-`, `chunk-` and `styles-` file and the fonts under `/media/`) is prefetched, the workers, receipt fonts and other images are cached the first time they are asked for, every app route is a navigation served from `index.html` (never `/api/`, `/mcp`, `/.well-known/`, `/actuator/` or a file), and only public answers that are the same for everyone are kept (`public-content`, freshness with 5 s: the home content, news and projects, 100 for 30 days; `payment-details`, freshness with 5 s, a day; `public-photos`, performance: `/api/content/media/*`, the photos the visitor has already seen, 300 at most, Q8); `backend/.../ServiceWorkerCachedAnswersTest` proves each cached path answers alike with no token, a donor's and an administrator's and sets no cookie, and that the data group URLs find no other GET handler, and `scripts/ngsw-config.test.mjs` which built files land in which group. `service-worker-bypass.ts` (`serviceWorkerBypassInterceptor`, the innermost interceptor of `appHttpClient`) adds `ngsw-bypass: true` to every `/api` request that is not a `GET` of an anchored public answer (`mayComeFromServiceWorker`), because Angular matches a data group URL anywhere in the address; `versionInterceptor` ignores the version headers of an answer that may be a copy the worker kept (`mayComeFromServiceWorker` while `SERVICE_WORKER_UPDATES.isEnabled`), so a weeks-old copy never names an old generation. `core/install/app-install.ts` (`AppInstall`): `way()` is `prompt` while the browser offered `beforeinstallprompt` (kept and prevented), `ios-share` on an iPhone or iPad, `mac-dock` in Safari on a Mac, `none` once installed or when the page runs as the app (`(display-mode: standalone)` or `navigator.standalone`); `offered()` is false for 90 days after "Teď ne" (`localStorage` `nfnn.install-offer.hidden-until`). `core/auth/app-entry-guard.ts`: `/aplikace`, the manifest's `start_url` and `id`, sends a signed-in administrator to `/sprava`, a signed-in donor to `/darce/prehled` and anyone else to `/` without opening the sign-in dialog (#349: a modal at launch focused the e-mail field and raised the keyboard; the header's "Přihlásit se" offers the sign-in, and the experts of #349 kept the role-based start after the app was closed instead of reopening the last screen), after one session refresh when the `nfnn.donor-session` flag says a session may exist. The built `ngsw-worker.js` begins with the site's payment route (`frontend/service-worker/payment-route.js`, prepended by `scripts/wrap-service-worker.mjs`, the `postbuild` of `npm run build`, once, which fails when a build with `ngsw.json` keeps a worker without it; the registered address and the Trusted Types policy stay the same, and the stack probe checks that the image's worker begins with it): a `message` and a `fetch` listener registered before Angular's, which take only their own message and the same-origin path `/platba.spayd` and stop the event there, never the network and nothing under `/api/`. The message `{ type: 'nfnn-payment-file', payment }` with a SPAYD text (`SPD*1.0*` and the QR alphanumeric characters `spayd()` writes, at most 1 000 characters) reserves it in the worker's memory for 10 s under a random id (at most 16 kept, the oldest giving way), answered on the message's port (`id: null` for anything else); `GET /platba.spayd?id=<id>`, the id as its only parameter, serves that text once as `platba.spayd` (`Content-Disposition: inline`, `no-store`, `nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox allow-downloads; frame-ancestors 'none'`), any other address 404 (a navigation 204, so a tab whose worker restarted between the reservation and the tap stays on the payment panel with its notice) and any other method 405, both as sandboxed plain text; so the address never carries a payment, and a link from elsewhere cannot make the site serve one. `scripts/service-worker-payment-route.test.mjs` runs the route in a VM and the wrap, and `e2e/pwa/payment-route.spec.ts` proves it behind the real nginx.
- `core/push/` (slice 2.28): push goes through Angular's own `ngsw-worker.js` and `SwPush`, never a worker of the site's own (Trusted Types admit only `/ngsw-worker.js`). `service-worker-push.ts` holds the token `SERVICE_WORKER_PUSH` (`NO_PUSH` by default, so development, specs and a browser without a worker take one path) with `isEnabled`, `controllerChanges`, `messages`, `notificationClicks` (`{action, data}`), `requestPermission()` (asks `Notification.requestPermission()` at once, so it runs inside the click's gesture, and answers `granted`, `denied` or `default`), `requestSubscription(publicKey)` → `{endpoint, p256dh, auth}` (`devicePushSubscriptionOf`, from `PushSubscription.toJSON().keys`, refused without them; a subscription the browser still holds under another `applicationServerKey`, as after the server replaced its push key, is ended first, because the browser refuses to subscribe again over it), `browserSubscription()` (`subscribed`, `not-subscribed` or `unknown`) and `unsubscribe()` (a device that was not subscribed counts as done). The last two and the check before subscribing read `navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistration()` → `pushManager.getSubscription()` themselves instead of `SwPush`, whose calls wait for a worker that controls the page and so never settle after a hard reload or with the server's switch off; each gives up after `PUSH_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 s), so a button that waits on one always settles; `core/updates/angular-service-worker.ts`, the only file that imports `@angular/service-worker`, provides it from `SwPush`, enabled only while a worker controls the page and the browser has `PushManager` and `Notification`. ngsw itself shows the notification of a message (`notification` of the payload) and on a click focuses the open app or opens the payload's `onActionClick` address. `alert-routes.ts` is the app's own closed table of the kinds (`test` → `/sprava/upozorneni`, `bank-import-*` → `/sprava`, `backups-*` → `/sprava/zalohy`, `mail-*` → `/sprava/nastaveni`, anything else → `/sprava/upozorneni`) and reads a message's `count` (a whole number 0–999, `MAX_OPEN_ALERT_COUNT`, the backend's `PushMessage.MAX_COUNT`, else nothing); its kinds are the shared cases `shared-cases/push/notification.json` (kind → tag and route), which `alert-routes.spec.ts` runs and `e2e/push-device.ts` reads its messages from. `alert-notification-clicks.ts` (`AlertNotificationClicks.start()`) routes a click the open app hears with `Router.navigateByUrl` by the kind alone, never to an address taken from the message, so the unsaved-changes guard still asks, and only in the tab that is visible and has the focus (ngsw focuses one tab and then tells every tab, so the others stay where they are); `app-badge.ts` (`AppBadge.start()`, `show(count)`) sets `navigator.setAppBadge(count)` or `clearAppBadge()` for each message with a count (the test alert carries the open count too) and when the alerts page loads, feature-detected and never throwing, and clears the badge when the signed-in administrator signs out or loses the role (`AuthStore.isAdmin` turning false). ngsw never sets a badge, so a closed app's badge shows what it knew when it was last open: the administration shell refreshes it (`features/admin/shell/alert-badge-refresh.ts`, `AlertBadgeRefresh.watch`) with one `GET /api/admin/operations/push` when the shell starts and again when the tab becomes visible, at most once a minute (`ALERT_BADGE_REFRESH_GAP_MS`), a failure ignored. Both start from an app initializer in `app.config.ts`. `push-availability.ts` tells the alerts page why a device cannot subscribe.
- `core/errors/`: `error-vocabulary.ts` holds the closed lists a browser error report may carry (`ERROR_KINDS` `unexpected`, `chunk-load`, `pdf-render`, `pdf-load`, `qr-encode`, `verify-parse`, `verify-signature`, `boot`, `sw-install`, `sw-unrecoverable`; `ERROR_PLACES` `global`, `navigation`, `start-up`, `service-worker`; `ERROR_NAMES`, the standard error and `DOMException` names plus `HttpErrorResponse` and `Other`; `BROWSERS`, `OPERATING_SYSTEMS`, `DISPLAY_MODES`), and `npm run errors:vocabulary` (`scripts/export-error-vocabulary.mjs`) writes them to `error-vocabulary.json`, which the spec keeps equal to the TypeScript and the backend's `ClientErrorVocabularyTest` to its enums. `client-platform.ts` (`detectPlatform`) turns the user agent into a browser family, its major version (1–999, else none) and an OS family, nothing more; `app-revision.ts` reads `NFNN_APP_REVISION`, which the image's build defines (`--define`), and keeps it only when it is what the server accepts (`revisionOf`: 7–40 lowercase hex digits or `dev`), `dev` otherwise, so a build with an odd revision never makes every report a 400. `error-reporter.ts` (`ErrorReporter.report(kind, place, error?)`) never throws: it keeps the error's `name` only when it is in `ERROR_NAMES` (`Other` otherwise) and never reads its message or stack, sends one report per kind, place and name every 10 minutes and counts the repeats it held back into the next one's `count` (at most 100), sends at most 10 per page load, keeps up to 20 while `Connectivity.browserSaysOffline()` says offline and sends them on the window's `online` event, and posts with a raw `fetch` (`keepalive`, `credentials: 'omit'`, no interceptor, a failure ignored) to `/api/operations/client-errors`. `reporting-error-handler.ts`: `ReportingErrorHandler` extends Angular's `ErrorHandler`, keeps its console output and reports `unexpected`/`global`, except a failed chunk (`isChunkLoadError`), which `ChunkLoadRecovery` reports itself, so a lost chunk is one report per page load; `app.config.ts` provides it as `appErrorHandler` (`app.config.spec.ts` checks `appConfig.providers` holds it). `ChunkLoadRecovery.handleNavigationError` reports `chunk-load`/`navigation` for a failed chunk. The receipt PDF (`ReceiptPdf`: `pdf-load`, `pdf-render`) and the verification page (`ReceiptVerifier`: `verify-signature`; `QrReader`: `qr-encode`) report their failures with place `global` (below); their specs assert the exact closed body through `core/errors/testing/client-error-reports.ts`. Specs: `error-reporter.spec.ts` (the canary `Error('Zxqv Kanárek 7781,77 Kč 2099-778177')` arrives as closed values only, dedupe, the per-load limit, the offline queue, the allowlist), `client-platform.spec.ts`, `error-vocabulary.spec.ts`, `reporting-error-handler.spec.ts`; `app-revision.spec.ts`; Playwright `e2e/client-errors.spec.ts` (an error thrown on the privacy page posts one report of closed values; a lost chunk posts exactly one `chunk-load` report per page load and no `unexpected` one; a receipt font gone after a deploy posts one `pdf-load` report named `NotFoundError`, and the page loads once more and finishes the download).
- `app.routes.ts`: `''` → `layout/public-shell` (header, footer) with children `'obnova-hesla'` → `features/donors/password-reset/` (title "Nové heslo | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'potvrzeni-emailu'` → `features/donors/email-verification/` (title "Potvrzení e-mailu | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'pripojeni'` → `features/donors/connect-app/` (title "Připojení aplikace | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'prvni-nastaveni'` → `features/operations/setup/first-setup` (title "První nastavení | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'overit'` → `features/donations/verification/verification-page` (title "Ověření potvrzení o daru | Nová naděje – nadační fond") and `''` → `features/content/content.routes.ts` (`''` home, `'zasady-ochrany-osobnich-udaju'` privacy policy, `'aktuality'` and `'projekty'` → `features/content/list/content-list` with route data `kind` (titles "Aktuality | Nová naděje – nadační fond", "Projekty | …"), `'aktuality/:slug'` and `'projekty/:slug'` → `features/content/detail/content-detail` with route data `kind` `news` / `project` (the tab title becomes "<title> | Nová naděje – nadační fond" once loaded), `'**'` not-found; a route cannot have both `children` and `loadChildren`, so the content routes hang under a child of their own); `'darce'` → `layout/donor-shell` with `features/donors/donors.routes.ts` behind `core/auth/donor-guard.ts`: one parent `''` → `features/donors/dashboard/donor-area` (the donor area) with the sections `'prehled'` (title "Můj přehled | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'dary'` ("Moje dary | …"), `'potvrzeni'` ("Potvrzení o daru | …"), `'ucty'` ("Bankovní účty | …"), `'udaje'` ("Údaje a zabezpečení | …") and `''` redirecting to `'prehled'`; `'sprava'` → the same `donor-shell` with `features/admin/admin.routes.ts` (`''` → `features/admin/home/`, title "Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'platby'` → `features/admin/payments/`, title "Platby z banky | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'spravci'` → `features/admin/administrators/`, title "Správci | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'starsi-symboly'` → `features/admin/symbol-claims/`, title "Odpovědi o starších symbolech | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'historie'` → `features/admin/actions/`, title "Historie změn | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'prehledy'` → `features/admin/report/`, title "Měsíční e-mail | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'zalohy'` → `features/admin/backups/`, title "Zálohy | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'upozorneni'` → `features/admin/alerts/`, title "Upozornění na telefon | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'logy'` → `features/admin/logs/`, title "Logy a diagnostika | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'nastaveni'` → `features/admin/settings/`, title "Nastavení | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'aktuality'` → `features/admin/news/admin-news`, title "Aktuality | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'aktuality/nova'` and `'aktuality/:id'` → `features/admin/news/admin-news-editor`, titles "Nová aktualita | Správa | …" and "Úprava aktuality | Správa | …"; `'projekty'` → `features/admin/projects/admin-projects`, title "Projekty | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'projekty/nova'` and `'projekty/:id'` → `features/admin/projects/admin-project-editor`, titles "Nový projekt | Správa | …" and "Úprava projektu | Správa | …"; `'darci'` → `features/admin/donors/`, title "Dárci | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'darci/:id'` → `features/admin/donor-detail/`, title "Dárce | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond") behind `core/auth/admin-guard.ts`. `'donor'` redirects to `'darce'` with the rest of the path (the earlier English address). Every address a visitor sees is Czech without diacritics; only `/api/...` stays English. Unknown addresses keep their URL and show `not-found`. nginx answers exactly these addresses with the app and status 200 (the map `$app_page` of `frontend/nginx.conf`, one line per route, a parameter as `[^/]+`, no trailing slash, because the router shows `not-found` for `/o-nas/` too) and every other path that is no file with the same `index.html` and status 404 (issue #350, Jakub 2026-11-06: nothing changes for the visitor, only the status is right); `app.routes.spec.ts` walks these routes (lazy children loaded, a prefix redirect such as `'donor'` taking the target's addresses) and fails unless the list equals the map, read by `scripts/app-routes.mjs` through the global setup `src/testing/server-routes-setup.ts`, so a new route adds its line there in the same change.
- `core/device/store/` (plan v3, 2.14; how it is secured: `docs/security-overview.md`, "Device copies"): `device-storage.ts` (the `DeviceStorage` port: device salt, registry entries, session keys and sealed records, every `write(change)` atomic; the database `nfnn.device`), `idb-device-storage.ts` (the only importer of idb, `eslint-confinement.js`; four object stores, one `readwrite` transaction per change, opening given 5 s, `blocking` closes the connection so another tab can delete the database, `destroy()` deletes it; `idbDeviceStoragePresent()` asks `indexedDB.databases()` so a sign-out never makes an empty database), `memory-device-storage.ts` (the same in memory, for memory-only mode), `account-store.ts` (`AccountStore` of one open copy and feed: `load()` → `empty`, `loaded` or `damaged`, `replace(contents)` seals one record per chunk and one for the summary under random record ids, the signal `contents`), `device-vault.ts` (`DeviceVault`, root; from 2.19 also the administration's copy: `administration` (the open copy, `reason` `sign-in`, `empty` or `unlocked`), `stores`, `administrationPasskeys`, `unlockAdministrationWithPasskey` (the PRF ceremony through `PASSKEY_READER`, outside the vault lock, and nothing opens when a sign-in or sign-out came in between), `unlockAdministrationWithPassword`, `lockAdministration`, `administrationRevoked` (deletes the administration copy and keeps the donor's), closed whenever `AdministrationAccess` locks; `signedIn`, `restored`, `signedOut`, `signedOutElsewhere`, `sessionLost`, `forget`, `passwordChanged`, `passwordRemoved`, `passkeyAdded`, `prfInput`, `replaceContents`, `withCopyLock`; `signedIn` and `restored` take `storeNothing`, which `AuthStore` sets for a `PUBLIC` session (2.10), so a "Cizí počítač" copy lives only in memory, also after a reload; signals `opened` (`{ store, reason: 'sign-in' | 'restored' | 'empty', account }`), `mode` (`stored` | `memory`) and `locked` (a copy of the account is on the device but this session cannot open it); tokens `DEVICE_STORAGE_OPENER`, `DEVICE_STORAGE_PRESENT`, `PASSWORD_KEY_DERIVATION`, `COPY_CHANNELS`, `STORAGE_PERSISTENCE`; Web Locks `nfnn.device-vault` and `nfnn.device-copy-write.<id>`, `BroadcastChannel` `nfnn.device-copy.<id>`, `localStorage` flag `nfnn.device-copy`), `testing/raw-scan.ts` (every stored value as text, its bytes and its base64, hex and URL decodings, for the canaries) and `testing/fake-device.ts` (named Web Locks and a channel bus for two tabs in one spec). `core/auth/session-device.ts` is what `AuthStore` tells the device (`SESSION_DEVICE`, a no-op by default, so specs and pages without the vault change nothing; `deviceAccountOf(donor)` with the passkey user handle of the donor's id and `administrator` for the `ADMIN` role); `core/auth/administration-access.ts` (`AdministrationAccess`, root, 2.19: locked at every start, unlocked by a sign-in in this tab and by the lock panel, locked again by sign-out and after `ADMINISTRATION_LOCK_AFTER_MS`, five minutes, with the page hidden (`ACCESS_CLOCK` reads both `performance.now()` and `Date.now()`, and either clock having moved five minutes, or the wall clock having gone back, locks it, because the monotonic clock stops while Apple devices, Android and Linux sleep; checked again on `visibilitychange`, `resume` and `pageshow`), `keepsPage` set when a form is dirty as it locks); the app provides `device-copies.ts` (`DeviceCopies`), which loads the vault and `features/donations/device-copy/` with `import()` at the first session event, so neither is in the bundle that loads first, or already when the sign-in dialog opens (`prepare()`, 2.19), because a passkey sign-in waits only 1.5 s for the device's PRF input and a copy signed in without it gets no passkey wrap. `features/donations/device-copy/`: `donor-sync-api.ts` (`/api/donations/me/sync` and `/sync/chunks?ids=`, at most 64 ids), `donor-copy-sync.ts` (`DonorCopySync`, 2.15: syncs a copy whenever one opens, at sign-in, restored or empty, and on `sync()`, `reload()` (every chunk again) and `catchUp()` (nothing within 30 s of the last sync, and it joins a sync that runs); one sync at a time and at most one queued after it, which a `reload()` turns into a full one; under the copy lock it reads the stored copy, asks for the manifest and only the chunks whose digest changed, checks every digest with `canonical-rows.ts`, tries twice, gives each request 30 s (`SYNC_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, so the copy lock is never held for ever), refuses an answer whose `subject` is not the open copy's donor (unreported: the session changed, not the data), keeps the signature and portrait of every active receipt (fetched through `ReceiptsApi.image` and kept only when they give their digest) and stores the whole copy with `DeviceVault.replaceContents`; then compares the copy's own figures with its summary, downloads it whole again at most once an hour when they differ and reports `device-copy-mismatch` at most once an hour (both pauses kept in memory, so per open tab); signals `syncing`, `open`, `view`, `firstSync` and `keepsOnDevice`), `donor-copy.ts` (`DonorCopyLink`, root: the `DonorCopy` the pages read, set by `DonorCopySync` when `device-copies.ts` loads it, so the donor pages reach the copy without importing the vault), `donor-copy-view.ts` (pure: `donorCopyView(contents)` turns the stored chunks into the summary, the gifts as `DonationItem`s in the order the API lists them (purpose text, sender account by `bankAccountId`, confirmed symbol by `symbolClaimId`, receipt number of the active single receipt, `receiptAvailable`), the issued receipts, the kept images and `agrees`; `summarizeDonations` adds up gifts as the server's summary does; `activeReceiptOf`, `imageDigestsOf`) and `copy-receipt-source.ts` (`copyBackedReceiptSource`: a receipt is issued online as before, and only when the server cannot be reached (status 0, or a gateway status without a problem body) the copy's active receipt of that year or gift is drawn; an image the copy keeps is read from it, any other from the server; the PDF is drawn again each time and never stored), `donor-donations-repository.ts` (`DonorDonationsRepository`, the one read of the donor's gifts for the pages, Donor pages below) and `testing/fake-donor-copy.ts` (`FakeDonorCopy` and `copyViewOf` for the page specs).
- `core/seo/canonical-link.ts` (above) and `core/auth/`: `donor.ts` (DTOs `Donor`, `RegisterDonorRequest`, `AuthResponse`, `PASSWORD_MIN_LENGTH` 12 shared by registration and reset), `auth-api.ts` (`/api/donors/auth/{register,login,login/second-factor,login/second-factor/recovery,refresh,logout}`, `email-verification/{request,confirm}`, `password-reset/{request,confirm}`, `/api/donors/me`, `/api/donors/me/password`, `/api/donors/me/email-change`, `/api/donors/me/second-factor/{setup,confirm,disable}`, `describeClient` → `/api/donors/oauth/client`, `authorize` → `/api/donors/oauth/authorize`, `connections` and `disconnect` → `/api/donors/me/connections[/{id}]`; `passkeySignInOptions` and `signInWithPasskey` → `/api/donors/auth/passkey[/options]`, `passkeys`, `passkeyCreationOptions(confirmation)`, `addPasskey` and `removePasskey` → `/api/donors/me/passkeys[/options|/{id}]`, `removePassword` → `/api/donors/me/password/remove`, `passkeyRecoveryOptions` and `recoverWithPasskey` → `/api/donors/auth/password-reset/passkey[/options]` (DTOs `PasskeyCreationOptions`, `PasskeySignInOptions`, `PasskeyRegistrationCredential`, `PasskeyAssertionCredential`, `Passkey`); the DTOs `ClientDescription`, `AuthorizationRequest`, `RedirectResponse`, `ConnectedApp`, `OAuthScope` in `donor.ts`), `auth-store.ts` (signals `donor`, `status`, `signedIn`, `isAdmin` (the session's roles contain `ADMIN`, which the backend grants only after the second factor); `register` opens no session, `confirmEmail(token)`, `completeSecondFactor(challenge, code)`, `changePassword`, `confirmSecondFactor(code)`, `disableSecondFactor` and `signInWithPasskey()` (options → the device → the answer) do, `setupSecondFactor(confirmation)` only returns the enrolment, `removePassword(confirmation)` adopts the new session, `confirmWith(password | null)` turns the password, or for an account without one the device's sign-in prompt over `/api/donors/auth/passkey/options`, into the `Confirmation` the credential changes send, `requestEmailChange` changes nothing until the link is opened; access token in memory; one refresh shared between callers, and between the tabs of the browser (plan v3, 2.8): the refresh runs under the Web Lock `nfnn-refresh` (`session-tabs.ts`: `TAB_LOCKS` is `navigator.locks`, null where the browser has none, which then refreshes without it), its request is cut off after 15 s (`REFRESH_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, the `XMLHttpRequest` aborted before the lock is released, so no other tab refreshes with the cookie a late answer would still replace) and a tab gives up waiting for the lock after 20 s (`REFRESH_LOCK_WAIT_MS`, longer than the request); the tab that refreshed announces the new session on the `BroadcastChannel` `nfnn-session` (`SESSION_CHANNEL`, a `session-refreshed` message of `version` 1 with the `AuthResponse`, checked by `isSessionRefreshed`, which also asks for the user's id and roles and the expiry, so a tab of another build after a deploy changes nothing with a message it would misread; posting never fails the refresh; the channel closed with the store), every other tab adopts it unless it holds the session of another donor; a refresh, once started, runs to its answer or its timeout even when its caller is gone (`share({ resetOnRefCountZero: false })`), because an aborted answer would be a lost one; `logout()` runs under the same lock (or, after 20 s of waiting, without it), its request cut off after 15 s like a refresh's so a stalled sign-out never holds the other tabs' refresh, then forgets the session and posts `signed-out`, on which every other tab forgets its own, and a tab that waited for the lock and meanwhile got one sends no refresh of its own (one that did not still presents the cookie the first tab's answer set, so the server sees a plain rotation); only a refusal (a 4xx other than 408 and 429) ends the session in the tab and drops the flag, and nothing else the browser keeps; a refresh without an answer (no connection, the timeout, the lock wait) keeps the session and the flag, and a `restore()` that got none leaves the visitor anonymous until the next `restore()`, which tries again at once after a quick failure and only after 10 s (`SLOW_RESTORE_PAUSE_MS`) after a timeout or a lock wait that gave up, so the guard of the first page does not keep a blank page waiting a second time; `restore()` on start calls `refresh` only when the `localStorage` flag `nfnn.donor-session` says a session may exist; session kinds (plan v3 2.10): `session-jar.ts` gives `SESSION_JAR`, the browser's jar (`nfnn-refresh`, `nfnn-session`, `nfnn.donor-session`) or, when the app runs installed (`runsInstalled`: `display-mode: standalone` or `navigator.standalone`), the app's (`nfnn-refresh-app`, `nfnn-session-app`, `nfnn.donor-session-app`), so the installed app and a tab of the same browser keep their own lock, channel and flag, and `auth-api.ts` sends `Nfnn-Session: APP` on the app's refresh and sign-out, so the backend reads and clears the app's own cookie; `login`, `completeSecondFactor` and `signInWithPasskey` take the kind the dialog chose (`BROWSER`, `REMEMBERED` or `PUBLIC`; the app always sends `APP`, an e-mail confirmation `BROWSER`), and the store keeps the answer's `session()`; a `PUBLIC` session keeps its flag in `sessionStorage` instead, so nothing of it outlives the browser, and `storesNothing()` tells the device copy (2.14) to keep nothing on the device; because `sessionStorage` belongs to one tab, a tab opened from a public-computer session starts signed out and offers the sign-in again, which is accepted for a borrowed computer), `auth-interceptor.ts` (Bearer on `/api/**` except the auth endpoints and a request whose `HttpContext` sets `WITHOUT_ACCESS_TOKEN` (the public receipt verification, the payment details and the setup status), which goes out without the header and is never refreshed; parses an `application/problem+json` Blob body into the `HttpErrorResponse` first; on 401 `auth/expired-token` (and, since 2.9, `auth/session-ended`, a token of a device signed out elsewhere, whose refresh then fails and forgets the session, a sign-out for the device copy, 2.14) refreshes once and retries; a request whose `HttpContext` sets `WITHOUT_ACCESS_TOKEN` goes without the Bearer header and so never refreshes, which `payment-details-api.ts` uses because that public answer is the same for everyone and the backend answers an expired token with 401 even there), `donor-guard.ts` (waits for the restore, else goes home and opens the sign-in dialog with `returnTo` set to the asked address), `login-dialog.service.ts` (`open(mode = 'login', { returnTo, afterSignIn, email })`, `LoginDialogMode` `login` | `register` | `reset`, read once by the dialog as its initial tab, `email` prefilling that tab's address (the first setup passes the administrator's); `keepRegistrationDraft`/`registrationDraft`/`dropRegistrationDraft` hold the registration fields (never the password) of a dialog closed half way; the dialog calls `signedIn()` after a sign-in (with or without the second factor), which closes it and then opens `returnTo` when given, else stays on the page and calls `afterSignIn` (Jakub 2026-10-23: a sign-in from the home page stays there, the header then offers "Můj přehled"; only a sign-in asked for by a protected page returns to it). `returnTo` comes from the guards, from the session-loss handlers of the overview and of every administration page (`router.url` before going home) and from the password-reset page (`/darce/prehled`)), `passkey-authenticator.ts` (`PasskeyAuthenticator.supported()`, `create(options)` and `get(options)` over `navigator.credentials` with base64url both ways; a refusal becomes `PasskeyCeremonyError` with `cancelled` (`NotAllowedError`, `AbortError`), `already-registered` (`InvalidStateError`), `unsupported` or `failed`; specs replace it with `testing/FakePasskeyAuthenticator`), `device-name.ts` (`deviceName(document)`: iPhone, iPad, Android, Mac, Windows, Chromebook or Linux from the user agent, the default name of a new key), `core/navigation/external-navigation.ts` (`ExternalNavigation.assign(url)` leaves the app for another site; specs replace it), `api-problem.ts` (`describeProblem(failure, generic)`: title, detail and field messages in one line, or `generic`; `problemOf` also reads the second-factor `challenge`), `testing/` fixtures (`sampleDonor`, `secondFactorRequired`, `secondFactorEnrolment`).

## Shared UI (`shared/ui/`)

- `form-field/`: `<app-form-field label [hint] [messages]>` around one control marked `appFormControl` (needs `id` and `ngModel`); renders `<label for>`, the hint and the message for the first error key (or the error's own text when `messages` has no entry for it, as the server's `{ server: message }`), sets `aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby` once touched or submitted (touched during a pointer press only from `pointerup` or `pointercancel` on, so a message that appears on the blur cannot push the submit button away before the click; a message already shown stays during a press; a required field the visitor only passed through without typing says nothing until the form is sent: its own blur marks it untouched again, so the message cannot move the form while the next field is tapped (on iOS the press ends before the focus moves, so the press guard cannot help there), while a field the app marks touched itself, as `placeServerErrors` or a refused publish date do, shows it; with the on-screen keyboard open (`SoftKeyboard.isOpen()`), Enter in a field with `enterkeyhint="next"` moves the focus to the next text field of the form instead of sending it, unless the whole form is already valid, as after the password manager filled it in; with a hardware keyboard Enter sends the form as on any web page (#359, #384)); `form[appFocusFirstInvalid]` focuses the first invalid control on submit; `server-errors.ts` `placeServerErrors(form, errors, controlNames)` puts the field errors of a 400 problem on their controls (touched, cleared by the next edit) and hands back those without a field. Control look is the global `.form-control` in `src/styles/_form-controls.scss`; components only space fields.
- `password-reveal/`: eye button "Zobrazit heslo" (`aria-pressed`, `aria-controls`) switching the input between `password` and `text`; every password field uses it.
- `account-username/`: `<app-account-username [email]>` is a hidden, read-only `input type="email" autocomplete="username"` (`tabindex="-1"`, `aria-hidden`) holding the account's e-mail. Every form that asks for the current password without an e-mail field (the account card's e-mail change, password change, password removal and second factor on and off, the passkeys panel's new key) puts it before its first field, as Chrome and Safari ask of such forms: a password manager then knows the account the password belongs to and fills no other field (Safari ignores `autocomplete="off"` and took "Název klíče" for the user name, Jakub 2026-10-31).
- `live-notice/`: `<app-live-notice [notice]>` takes a `Notice` (`{ kind: 'status' | 'alert', text }` or `null`) and keeps a `role="status"` and a `role="alert"` region rendered, showing the text as `.ui-notice--status` or `--alert` in the matching one, so a screen reader announces it; every admin page's action line uses it.
- `copy-button/`: `<app-copy-button [value] what="číslo účtu">`: "Kopírovat" plus the hidden `what` (an optional `label` replaces both with a visible label of its own, as "Zkopírovat zprávu pro technika"); says "Zkopírováno" for 2 s and in a `role="status"` line (an optional `copiedStatus` gives that line a sentence of its own); a visible sentence when the browser refuses (`failedStatus` for one of its own), under the button aligned to the right (`align="start"`: to the left). Its look is the global `.ui-pill-button` (`--done` while it says "Zkopírováno"), which `share-button` shares.
- `share-button/`: `<app-share-button [pageTitle] [url]>` (v3 2.26a): where the browser has the Web Share API and `navigator.canShare({ title, url })` does not refuse (or is missing), one "Sdílet" pill with a share icon that calls `navigator.share({ title, url })`, ignoring presses while the share sheet is open (`aria-disabled`); the visitor closing the sheet (`AbortError`) says nothing, any other refusal shows the `role="alert"` line "Sdílení se nepodařilo. Odkaz na stránku si můžete zkopírovat." with the copy button beside it, both kept until a share succeeds (so trying again and closing the sheet keeps the offer); each refusal renders the alert as a new element (an `@for` keyed by the count of refusals), so a screen reader hears a second refusal too, even one the browser answers at once. One `<app-copy-button>` serves both the fallback and the offer after a refusal. Without the API, or when `canShare` refuses, only `<app-copy-button>` "Kopírovat odkaz", whose status line says "Odkaz je zkopírovaný, můžete ho vložit do zprávy." and, when the browser refuses to copy, "Zkopírovat se nepodařilo. Odkaz najdete v adresním řádku prohlížeče." (the link is not written on the page), aligned to the left. Nothing loads from elsewhere and the backend is not asked. In a list of articles (v3 2.26) `[subject]` names the article: the Sdílet button and the copy button carry it as visually hidden text after their label ("Sdílet Zahrada pro děti"; the copy button's `labelDetail`), so every button of a feed has a name of its own, and `tone="dark"` (host class `is-on-dark`, also on `copy-button` as `tone`) puts its status and refusal lines in the on-image colour over a photo. Texts under `ui.share.*`; spec `share-button.spec.ts`.
- `qr-code/`: `<app-qr-code [matrix] [variant] label>` draws a QR code from a module matrix (`qr-matrix.ts`: `QrMatrix`, `isDark`, `alignmentCentres`), the browser's own from `qr-encoder.ts` for every QR code (since 1.4 the backend sends none), as inline SVG with a 4-module quiet zone, `role="img"` named by `label`. `branded` (default, the payment look chosen by Jakub on 2026-10-23): round dots in `--color-qr-module`, rounded finder squares and alignment marks in `--color-qr-mark`, and over the centre a blue disc with the logo's star (`--color-logo`, `--color-logo-fill`; the matrix must be level H); when the matrix changes it morphs in a wave from the star outwards (kept dots pulse, gone ones shrink, new ones grow in the mark colour and settle to the module colour, ~2.6 s, Web Animations API, nothing under `prefers-reduced-motion`). `plain`: black squares, used for the authenticator link. `pngDataUrl(sidePx)` paints a copy with the token colours and returns a PNG made on a canvas. `qr-encoder.ts` is the browser's own QR encoder (no library), bit for bit the symbol ZXing 3.5.4 drew on the backend with the ISO-8859-1 hint (the backend checked it until 1.10 retired ZXing; the shared cases keep what it drew): `encodeText(text, level)` picks the mode as ZXing does (only digits numeric, the 45 alphanumeric characters alphanumeric, anything else ISO-8859-1 bytes behind the ECI 1 header; a character outside ISO-8859-1 is refused instead of written as `?`), takes the smallest version that holds the text at the level asked for (none above 40: refused), and keeps the first of the eight masks with the lowest ZXing penalty (rule 3 counts a finder-like run only when four light modules inside the symbol lie on one side; the edge is not light); `encodeSegments(segments, level)` takes segments built by hand (`byteSegment`, `numericSegment`, `alphanumericSegment`, `eciSegment`), for the verification link of a receipt (its prefix in bytes, its payload in digits, the level chosen by the receipt). It returns the matrix with its version, level and mask; about 3 ms for an authenticator link (version 9), about 40 ms at version 40. A text over 7089 characters, more than any QR code holds, is refused before any work. Besides the payment and authenticator cases it runs `shared-cases/qr/encoder.json`, 112 symbols ZXing encoded at every level, at versions 1 to 40 and in every mode, which the backend runs against ZXing too.
- `side-nav/`: `<app-side-nav [groups] label [navId] [panel]>` renders an area's two-column layout: a sticky panel left (`--side-nav-width` 240 px, below the top safe area, the whole layout at most `--side-nav-layout-max` 1320 px) with a `nav` (`aria-label` = `label`) of `SideNavGroup`s (`{ id, label | null, items: SideNavItem[] }`; a titled group's `ul` is `aria-labelledby` its title, an untitled one has no label) of `SideNavItem` links (`{ path, label, exact?, badge? }`, `routerLinkActive` with `aria-current="page"`, prefix match unless `exact`; a `badge` `{ count, description }` with a count above 0 adds an accent pill with the count (`aria-hidden`) and the `description` as visually hidden text, so the area passes `groups` as a signal-computed value when the count changes), and the projected content (`<ng-content />`, a `router-outlet`) right. With `[panel]="false"` (an area shell passes `!CompactLayout.isCompact()`) only the projected content stays, in the same `main#obsah`, so a page is not made again when the width crosses `nav`; the panel holds only links and no menu button (since 2.24 phones reach the sections from the account sheet in the donor area and from the overview's rows in the administration).
- `radio-group/`: `[appRadioGroup]` on a group of `role="radio"` buttons makes it a `radiogroup` that answers the arrow keys, Home and End by focusing and clicking the next choice; the buttons bind `[tabindex]` 0 for the chosen one and -1 for the rest (sign-in dialog: donor type and sign-in method; password reset: method).
- `markdown/`: `parseMarkdown(text)` (`markdown-parser.ts`) turns a news or project body into blocks (paragraph with line breaks, heading level 2 or 3, list, quote) of inline nodes (text, bold, italic, link, break), the subset in `docs/api-contract.md` ("`content` — public CMS"); a link keeps only an `http(s)`, `mailto:` or site-relative address, everything else, HTML included, stays text. `<app-markdown [text]>` renders the tree with its own elements through a recursive `ng-template` and `{{ }}` bindings (no `innerHTML`), in the prose look (70 ch, serif headings). `<app-markdown-toolbar [target]>` above a textarea: the `role="group"` "Formátování textu" with Tučně, Kurzíva, Nadpis, Odrážky, Číslování and Odkaz, each wrapping the selection or prefixing its lines (a placeholder word selected when nothing was), then focusing the textarea and dispatching `input` so `ngModel` follows; the field's hint says how to format and that the editor's "Náhled" shows the look (the body-only preview under the textarea went with the editors' whole preview, Jakub 2026-10-24). Texts under `ui.markdown.*`; specs beside each.
- `file-button/`: `<app-file-button inputId [accept] [describedBy] [disabled] (chosen)>` is a file choice that looks like a button: a visually hidden `input type=file` and its `<label class="ui-button">` with the projected text (the focus ring of the input drawn on the label); `chosen` emits the `File` and the input is reset, so the same file can be chosen again (the photo in the news and project editors, the signature scan).
- `download-progress/`: `<app-download-progress [progress] [compact] (cancelled)>` shows a `DownloadProgressState` (`running` with `step` of `steps`, its text and an optional `detail`, `ready`, `done`, `cancelled`) in an always rendered `role="status"` line (its own typography: `--text-sm`, `--text-xs` when `compact` as in the payments table, neutral while running, ready and cancelled, the success colour once done, `overflow-wrap: anywhere`; the caller sets the alignment on the host), a 12 rem `<progress>` (at most the width it gets, named by the text, with the `detail` as its `aria-valuetext` and shown beside it, hidden from the accessibility tree, so a detail that changes often, such as the page, is not announced) while running and "Zrušit" once it has run for 8 s (`CANCEL_OFFERED_AFTER_MS`); it has no button of its own for `ready`, because the button that started the download opens the ready file.
- `focal-point/` (slice 2.23b): `FocalPoint` (`{x, y}` in whole percent), `PHOTO_SHAPES` (each shape the site crops a content photo into, with its ratio and the position it used before a point existed: `newsCard`, `projectCard`, `articleTop`, and since 2.26 `newsFeatured` (12:5), `newsFeaturedMedium` (3:2) and `newsFeaturedTall` (4:5) for the leading news of Aktuality as wide, middle and upright as the card stands on a computer, a tablet and a phone, `newsCardSide` (1:1) for an older news card with its photo beside the text and `storyTile` for a story of the year; 2.23a adds the wide band and the square), `objectPosition(point, shape)` and the directive `img[appFocalPoint][focalShape]`, which binds the image's `object-position`; the news card, the project card and the article top use it, so a photo without a point keeps its earlier crop.
- `avatar/`: `<app-avatar [name] [src] [size] [srcset] [sizes] [loading]>` is the one place that draws a person's round picture: the portrait (`alt=""`, `width`/`height` = `size`, cover-cropped to a circle) or, without one, the initials of the first two words of `name` in capitals (Jakub 2026-10-31: "ZZ" for Zdeňka Znamenáčková); the whole host is `aria-hidden`, because the name always stands beside it. The parent's class sets its width, height and the `font-size` token of the initials; the theme gives the circle, the light surface behind it and the serif. Used by the home "Kdo fond vede" block and `/sprava/lide-fondu` (the list and the portrait preview).
- `dialog/`: `<app-dialog labelledBy [wide] [presentation] [pushesHistory] [dismissible] (dismissed)>` (`[dismissible]="false"` while a step runs: Esc is refused, × is disabled, the backdrop and Back do nothing, and a close the browser forces anyway opens it again) on a native `<dialog>` opened with `showModal()` (focus kept inside, page inert, focus to the `autofocus` control; Esc, × and backdrop emit `dismissed`; focus returns to the opener on destroy; full-screen up to 480 px). `presentation` (`dialog-presentation.ts`, plan v3 2.22) is `auto` (the default), `centered`, `sheet` or `full`, written to `data-presentation`: `auto` takes `DIALOG_PRESENTATION`, an injection token that an area shell may provide and that is `centered` (today's dialog) unless one does, so no dialog changes its look until the app shells of 2.23 and 2.24 provide `sheet`; at `bp.down(nav)` a sheet is anchored to the bottom (`--sheet-max-width`, at most the screen height less `--space-12`, its own scroll, top corners `--radius-sheet`, `--shadow-sheet`, the bottom padding above the safe area, a grabber with `aria-hidden` across the top of the sheet (`--space-8` high, the visible bar its `::before`), which a primary pointer drags down (2.24, `sheet-drag.ts`, `SheetDrag`: the panel follows the pointer through the CSS `translate` property, since the entrance animation holds `transform`, and closes like × once it moved at least the larger of 72 px and a quarter of its height, or on a flick of 0.5 px/ms after 24 px, otherwise it springs back; a finger still moving up as it lets go has changed its mind, and the sheet springs back however far it went (#382)); a finger also pulls the sheet by its content as on iOS (#382, below `nav` only, touch events because Safari cancels pointer events once it scrolls): `canPullSheetFrom` lets a touch pull only when the panel and every element between it and the touched one are scrolled to their top, no text is selected, and it did not start on the focused text field, a `textarea`, `select`, slider, `canvas`, `contenteditable` or `app-zoomable`, which keep their own gestures, and `pullIntent` decides after 10 px: mostly down (more than 1.2 times sideways) pulls the sheet, anything else (up, sideways, or a finger that rested 300 ms first) is left to the content for the rest of that touch, a second finger springs the sheet back, and a pulling touch calls `preventDefault`, so the content neither scrolls nor bounces and the keyboard directive below sees no scroll (an open keyboard stays until the sheet closes, the experts' choice for #382, so a sheet that springs back keeps its field and caret); the backdrop fades with the share of the sheet pulled away (`--dialog-pull`, also set from `[pulledAway]` by the zoom viewer); a release that closes it slides the sheet out over the panel's `translate` transition (inert meanwhile, so nothing in it takes a second press) and then emits `dismissed` as × does, so the history entry goes back the same way, and under `prefers-reduced-motion` the global rule leaves no transition, so it closes at once; a page that keeps it open gets it back in place; a dialog that is not dismissible, and one whose page sets `[unsavedInput]` (an opt-in for input that took effort and is kept nowhere else; no dialog sets it yet, since the registration keeps its draft and the other sheets hold a code or a tick), only stretches under the finger with growing resistance (`rubberBand`, at most 96 px) and springs back, as iOS does under `isModalInPresentation`; a release of the second kind that would have closed it opens a nested sheet `role="alertdialog"` "Zahodit rozepsané údaje?" with "Pokračovat v psaní" (focused, also Esc, Back and its own pull) and "Zahodit", which closes the sheet; ×, Esc, the backdrop and Back close without asking, as on iOS, where only the gesture is guarded; the × stays the way that needs no gesture, WCAG 2.5.7), the side padding at least the side safe area, and the action row `.ui-dialog__actions` (`_dialog.scss`) sticky at the bottom of the sheet above the bottom safe area, so the buttons of a long sheet stay in reach, the × kept, WCAG 2.5.7) and `full` fills the screen; above `nav` both are the centered dialog. While the on-screen keyboard is open (`core/layout/soft-keyboard.ts`; `keyboard-avoidance.ts`, the directive `KeyboardAvoidance` on the `<dialog>`, #359) the dialog keeps the part of the screen the keyboard covers as its bottom padding (`--keyboard-covered-height`, `_dialog.scss`; the full-screen phone dialog fills it with the surface colour, a sheet sits above the keyboard), so its whole content can be scrolled above the keyboard, and the focused field is scrolled the least way into the visible part (16 px clear of its edges), together with its form's submit button when both fit, on focus and whenever the visible part changes; a one-finger drag that scrolls the dialog with the keyboard open (one that starts on a text field, uses two fingers or comes after the finger let go does not) moves the focus to the panel (`tabindex="-1"` until the focus leaves it), which closes the keyboard as a native form sheet does (#395) and keeps the focus inside the dialog. Every dialog keeps a history entry (2.22): on open it calls `DialogHistory.open` with a listener of its own, Back over that entry emits `dismissed`, and when the page keeps the dialog open (a running restore, erasure, pairing or withdrawal refuses to close) it asks for the entry again after the next render, so the following Back still lands on the dialog and not on the previous page; on destroy it gives its own entries back with `close(listener)`, leaving those of a dialog opened over it. `[pushesHistory]="false"` is set by the two dialogs whose history is kept elsewhere: the sign-in dialog, whose `LoginDialogService` adds entries for its steps and hands its entry over to the page it was opened for, and the unsaved-changes dialog, which exists only while a navigation waits on its guard (an entry pushed then would race the router restoring the address when the visitor stays). The panel ends with a visually hidden `role="status"` region, rendered with the dialog and empty, which shows `DialogAnnouncer.message()` (`dialog-announcer.ts`, root): while a modal dialog is open the page behind it is inert and out of the accessibility tree, so a message from behind it (the chunk-load bar) is announced only from inside the open dialog; `announce(text)` returns the function that withdraws it, which leaves a later announcement standing. `dialog-history.ts` (`DialogHistory`, root service) gives every dialog the browser's Back: `open(listener)` pushes one entry for the same address (state key `nfnnDialogDepth`), deferred until a running navigation ends (`router.currentNavigation()` is null), so a guard-opened dialog adds it on the page it redirected to; `showStep(depth, listener?)` adds or takes back entries for further steps of that dialog (the sign-in dialog names its own, so a dialog over it keeps its entry on top); Back over its own entry calls the listener's `stepBack()` or `close()`, and a jump past the page closes the dialog and leaves the navigation to the router; `close(listener?)` takes that dialog's entries back with `history.go(-n)` (without a listener, the top dialog's), and Back tells the listeners only once the stack is settled, so a listener that closes itself on Back adds no entry; an entry that carries the depth when no dialog owns one (a reload with a dialog open, Forward onto the entry of a closed dialog) is never left as a dead Back press: `app.config.ts` starts `DialogHistory` before the first page opens (`dialogHistoryAtStart`), which takes such an entry back unseen by the router, and a dialog opened on such an entry adopts it instead of adding another; it keeps a stack of open dialogs (2.24), each with its own entries: a dialog opened over another adds its entry on top, Back closes or steps back only the top one, a lower dialog that closes by itself takes back only its own entries and leaves the one above open, a jump back past several closes them top first, and a dialog that closes while another opens in the same moment (the pairing dialog giving way to "Mezitím se to změnilo") leaves exactly the new one's entry; `LoginDialogService` passes its own listener, so the sign-in dialog leaves another dialog's entry alone; a navigation a page starts itself while a dialog is open (`sendToSignIn` after a 401) leaves the dialog's entry under the new page, one extra Back press, until such a navigation hands the entry over like the sign-in dialog does; the fold at start strips the mark from the entry before it goes back, so the router's first navigation never copies it to the page below; if that traversal never arrived, the next Back would be taken as the fold's; the tests press Back from the page (`history.back()`), so a real Back button or swipe over the entry a refused close asks for again (which Chrome may skip as added without a click) is on Jakub's device checklist; `handOver(url)` replaces the top entry with the page a guard-opened dialog was opened for, which the router then replaces again (the router replaces an entry whose address is already the target), so Back from that page returns to the page under the dialog; `release()` forgets the entries without traversing (a link that opens another page in their place with `replaceUrl`), and any imperative navigation forgets them too, so closing later never leaves the page. Its popstate listener sits on `window` in the capture phase and is registered when the module loads, before Angular's `Location` registers its own: Chrome runs listeners on `window` in registration order, and the dialog's own entries must never reach the router, whose same-address `NavigationSkipped` would make `public-shell` move the focus to the page heading. `testing/fake-dialog-history.ts` records the calls for specs.
- `needs-connection/` (plan v3 2.7): `[appNeedsConnection]` on a control that needs the server (every submit button, every button without a `type`, every `ui-button--danger`/`--danger-soft` button and every button whose `(click)` calls a method of its component that subscribes, `firstValueFrom` or `lastValueFrom`, directly or through other methods of the component, checked by `scripts/needs-connection.mjs` in `npm run lint`; its exceptions in `WORKS_WITHOUT_CONNECTION`, a whole template or `template#handler`, each with its reason and reported once it names no control (a `template#handler` entry also clears a danger button whose click calls only listed handlers): the receipt code reader of `/overit`, which checks offline, the sheet's "Zkusit znovu", which is the probe, sign-out from the account sheet, which forgets the session on the device without the server, and the two panel switches of the first setup, and the dialog that deletes the data on this device, which is local and signs out the same way): while `Connectivity.offline()` it sets `aria-disabled="true"` (keeping a value it had), the class `needs-connection-offline`, and a hint `span.ui-needs-connection` "Potřebuje připojení" after the control, named in its `aria-describedby`; the control stays focusable and its click (capture phase) and the submit of its form (capture phase at the document) are swallowed, so nothing is sent and the text typed stays.
